- [ ] **Step 4: Implement `src/mutation/approval.py`**

```python
"""P13's `review_approval`, and §8.4's protected policy, as two gates.

Four rules, all enforced here and none of them decided by P13 (SPEC, Contract in
-> From P13):

 1. Only `verdict = approved` satisfies the policy.
 2. The approval must match the plan it is presented against -- same `plan_id`,
    same `placement_decision_ref`, same `plan_version`. A mismatch on any of the
    three is `review_policy_unsatisfied`, not an approval of a neighbouring plan.
 3. An approval never overrides another refusal. It lifts
    `review_policy_unsatisfied` and nothing else.
 4. P12 verifies; it does not present.

*"Absence is a refusal, never a default."* No approval record means
`review_policy_unsatisfied`. There is no timeout that ripens into consent and no
configuration that skips the check.

The record type lives here because P13 does not exist yet and P12's precondition
would otherwise have an enforcement rule and no input. It is a TYPE, not a
producer: `approval_for` is injected, and nothing under `src/mutation/` writes an
approval or appends P13's `apply review approval` event.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import sqlite3
from collections.abc import Callable
from dataclasses import dataclass

from placement.vocabulary import BLOCKED_PENDING_USER, REVIEW_REQUIRED
from privacy.classification_store import ClassificationRecord, ClassificationStore

from mutation.plan import MovePlan
from mutation.vocabulary import (
    APPROVED, PROTECTED_WITHOUT_POLICY, REFRESH_REQUIRED,
    REVIEW_POLICY_UNSATISFIED, REVIEW_VERDICTS, check,
)

#: The two P11 policies that demand an approval before execution. `auto_eligible`
#: is the third value and demands none.
REVIEW_DEMANDING_POLICIES: tuple[str, ...] = (REVIEW_REQUIRED, BLOCKED_PENDING_USER)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ReviewApproval:
    approval_id: str
    plan_id: str
    placement_decision_ref: str
    plan_version: str
    required_review_policy: str
    verdict: str
    presented_state_ref: str
    user_id: str
    decided_at: str

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        check(self.verdict, REVIEW_VERDICTS, name="review verdict")
        for name in ("approval_id", "plan_id", "placement_decision_ref",
                     "plan_version", "presented_state_ref", "user_id",
                     "decided_at"):
            if not getattr(self, name):
                raise ValueError(f"{name} is required on a review approval")


def approval_satisfies(plan: MovePlan, approval: ReviewApproval | None) -> bool:
    """Rules 1 and 2, together, because either alone admits the other's failure."""
    if approval is None:
        return False
    return (approval.verdict == APPROVED
            and approval.plan_id == plan.plan_id
            and approval.placement_decision_ref == plan.placement_decision_reference
            and approval.plan_version == plan.organization_plan_version)


def refresh_requested(plan: MovePlan, *,
                      approval_for: Callable[[str], ReviewApproval | None]) -> bool:
    """Did the user ask for the plan to be re-derived rather than applied?

    `refresh_required` routes to the same path as §8.3's five staleness triggers:
    the caller re-validates and supersedes the plan rather than applying an old
    decision to a changed file. The gate still refuses -- this is the signal that
    tells the sequencer to rebuild rather than to give up.
    """
    approval = approval_for(plan.plan_id)
    return approval is not None and approval.verdict == REFRESH_REQUIRED


def review_gate(*, approval_for: Callable[[str], ReviewApproval | None]
                ) -> Callable[[MovePlan], str | None]:
    """A gate for `ApplyContext.gates`. Returns a refusal class or None."""

    def gate(plan: MovePlan) -> str | None:
        if plan.required_review_policy not in REVIEW_DEMANDING_POLICIES:
            return None
        if approval_satisfies(plan, approval_for(plan.plan_id)):
            return None
        return REVIEW_POLICY_UNSATISFIED

    return gate


def classification_reader(conn: sqlite3.Connection
                          ) -> Callable[[MovePlan], ClassificationRecord | None]:
    """P7's authoritative record for a plan's exact file version (D2).

    Keyed on `(file_id, content_hash)`, which is what makes it the record for the
    version the plan expects rather than for whatever is at that path now.
    """
    store = ClassificationStore(conn)

    def read(plan: MovePlan) -> ClassificationRecord | None:
        return store.current(plan.file_id, plan.expected_content_hash)

    return read


def protected_gate(*,
                   classification_for: Callable[[MovePlan],
                                                ClassificationRecord | None],
                   movement_permitted_for: Callable[[MovePlan], bool]
                   ) -> Callable[[MovePlan], str | None]:
    """§8.4: protected material *"should not be moved automatically without a user
    policy that explicitly permits it"*.

    An ABSENT classification refuses. Never default an absent classification to a
    public or low class: with no detector present every file resolves to
    unclassified, and a default here would turn the safety rule inside out on a
    real corpus.

    Reads `ClassificationRecord.protected` and no handling-class set, because
    **C5** -- is `protected` exactly the top two handling classes? -- is open, and
    publishing that set would answer it in code.
    """

    def gate(plan: MovePlan) -> str | None:
        record = classification_for(plan)
        if record is None:
            return PROTECTED_WITHOUT_POLICY
        if record.protected and not movement_permitted_for(plan):
            return PROTECTED_WITHOUT_POLICY
        return None

    return gate
```

### Task 11: Conditional undo — reverse, or surface a conflict and touch nothing

**Files:**
- Create: `src/mutation/undo.py`
- Create: `tests/p12/test_p12_undo.py`

**Interfaces:**

*Consumes:* `mutation.journal.{JournalEntry, entry_by_id, is_reversed, append_journal_entry}`, `mutation.cross_volume.{perform_cross_volume, copy_exclusively, DestinationTaken}`, `mutation.context.ApplyContext`, `database_agent.{verify, files_table}`.

*Produces:*

```python
class NoSuchEntry(KeyError): ...
class AlreadyReversed(RuntimeError): ...
class NotAnAppliedEntry(ValueError): ...

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class UndoVerdict:
    entry_id: str
    verdict: str                          # one of UNDO_VERDICTS
    reversal_entry_id: str | None
    destination_path: str
    original_source_path: str
    expected_hash: str
    observed_destination_hash: str | None
    occupant_at_source_hash: str | None
    directory_outcomes: tuple[tuple[str, str], ...]
    detail: Mapping[str, object]

    @property
    def reversed_successfully(self) -> bool: ...

def undo(conn, entry_id: str, *, context: ApplyContext) -> UndoVerdict: ...
def undo_verdicts_for(conn, plan_id: str) -> tuple[UndoVerdict, ...]: ...
```

**Done-means:** **12** in full.

> **The five checks `66` §11 requires, in order.** *"Before reversing a move, the system verifies that the item at the destination is still the expected content, that its content hash has not changed, that restoring it will not overwrite another file, that the source location is available, and that no later user or external process has made the reversal unsafe."* Availability runs first because an unmounted volume makes every later answer meaningless; then presence, then content, then the source-path occupancy. A conflict performs **no mutation** and reports the relevant paths and hashes for manual resolution — *"This action cannot be undone automatically because the file changed after it was moved."* Undo never forces a rollback.

> **Reversal is itself a mutation and runs the full discipline.** V1 and V2 before, V3 after, and V4 where the reversal crosses volumes — the same code paths as a forward apply, because a reversal that skipped verification would be the one unverified write in the product. Its own journal entry is **appended**; the original entry is never edited or removed.

> **Task 12 fills `directory_outcomes`; Task 11 leaves it empty.** The file reversal and the directory reversal are two separable questions with two separable failure modes, and §8.3 is explicit that a retained directory *"is never a conflict, because the file reversal itself succeeded"*. Building them in one task would make it easy to write a directory failure into a file verdict.

Create `tests/p12/test_p12_undo.py`:

```python
"""§8.3's conditional undo. A conflict mutates nothing and reports both sides."""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
from pathlib import Path

import pytest

from mutation import vocabulary as v
from mutation.context import ApplyContext
from mutation.execute import apply_plan
from mutation.journal import entries_for_plan, entry_by_id, is_reversed
from mutation.undo import (
    AlreadyReversed, NoSuchEntry, NotAnAppliedEntry, UndoVerdict, undo,
    undo_verdicts_for,
)

from tests.p12.conftest import CONSTRAINTS, FIXTURE_NODES


def _context(landscape, ids, *, volume_of=None):
    return ApplyContext(
        constraints=CONSTRAINTS, nodes=FIXTURE_NODES,
        legal_destination_ids=frozenset({"n-course", "n-phys"}),
        high_level_folders=landscape, gates=(),
        suffix_for=lambda stem, attempt: f"{stem} ({attempt})",
        max_suffix_attempts=100, extra_protected=None,
        conflict_copies=lambda path: (), dataless_of=lambda path: False,
        volume_of=volume_of or (lambda path: "vol-main"),
        now=lambda: "2026-08-29T00:50:00Z", mint_id=ids,
        component_version="p12-test", scan_state="included", materialized=True)


@pytest.fixture()
def applied(p12_conn, planned, landscape, ids):
    plan, source = planned
    record = apply_plan(p12_conn, plan, context=_context(landscape, ids))
    assert record.result == v.APPLIED
    return plan, source, record, _context(landscape, ids)


def test_undo_of_an_untouched_move_restores_byte_identical_content(
        p12_conn, applied):
    plan, source, record, context = applied
    verdict = undo(p12_conn, record.journal_entry_id, context=context)
    assert verdict.verdict == v.REVERSED
    assert verdict.reversed_successfully is True
    assert source.read_bytes() == b"PHYS1401 syllabus"
    assert not Path(record.final_destination_path).exists()
    assert verdict.observed_destination_hash == plan.expected_content_hash


def test_undo_verifies_the_restored_file_and_records_v1_v2_v3(p12_conn, applied):
    plan, _, record, context = applied
    before = p12_conn.execute(
        "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM events WHERE event_type = 'hashing'").fetchone()[0]
    undo(p12_conn, record.journal_entry_id, context=context)
    points = [json.loads(row[0])["point"] for row in p12_conn.execute(
        "SELECT explanation FROM events WHERE event_type = 'hashing' "
        "ORDER BY event_id")][before:]
    assert points == ["V1", "V2", "V3"]


def test_undo_appends_a_reversal_entry_and_never_edits_the_original(
        p12_conn, applied):
    plan, _, record, context = applied
    original = entry_by_id(p12_conn, record.journal_entry_id)
    verdict = undo(p12_conn, record.journal_entry_id, context=context)
    assert entry_by_id(p12_conn, record.journal_entry_id) == original
    reversal = entry_by_id(p12_conn, verdict.reversal_entry_id)
    assert reversal.entry_kind == v.ENTRY_REVERSAL
    assert reversal.reverses_entry_id == record.journal_entry_id
    assert reversal.original_source_path == original.destination_path
    assert reversal.destination_path == original.original_source_path
    assert is_reversed(p12_conn, record.journal_entry_id) is True
    assert len(entries_for_plan(p12_conn, plan.plan_id)) == 2


def test_undo_appends_one_undo_event_with_both_paths(p12_conn, applied):
    plan, source, record, context = applied
    undo(p12_conn, record.journal_entry_id, context=context)
    row = p12_conn.execute(
        "SELECT subsystem, old_path, new_path FROM events WHERE event_type = ?",
        (v.UNDO,)).fetchone()
    assert tuple(row) == (v.SUBSYSTEM, record.final_destination_path, str(source))


def test_undo_updates_p1s_current_path_back_to_the_original(p12_conn, applied):
    plan, source, record, context = applied
    undo(p12_conn, record.journal_entry_id, context=context)
    assert p12_conn.execute(
        "SELECT current_path FROM files WHERE file_id = ?",
        (plan.file_id,)).fetchone()[0] == str(source)


def test_an_edited_destination_conflicts_and_mutates_nothing(p12_conn, applied):
    plan, source, record, context = applied
    destination = Path(record.final_destination_path)
    destination.write_bytes(b"the user edited it after it was filed")
    verdict = undo(p12_conn, record.journal_entry_id, context=context)
    assert verdict.verdict == v.CONFLICT_DESTINATION_CONTENT_CHANGED
    assert destination.read_bytes() == b"the user edited it after it was filed"
    assert not source.exists()
    assert verdict.destination_path == str(destination)
    assert verdict.original_source_path == str(source)
    assert verdict.expected_hash == plan.expected_content_hash
    assert verdict.observed_destination_hash != plan.expected_content_hash
    assert verdict.detail["message"] == (
        "This action cannot be undone automatically because the file changed "
        "after it was moved.")
    assert entries_for_plan(p12_conn, plan.plan_id) == \
        entries_for_plan(p12_conn, plan.plan_id)
    assert is_reversed(p12_conn, record.journal_entry_id) is False


def test_a_missing_destination_conflicts(p12_conn, applied):
    plan, source, record, context = applied
    Path(record.final_destination_path).unlink()
    verdict = undo(p12_conn, record.journal_entry_id, context=context)
    assert verdict.verdict == v.CONFLICT_DESTINATION_MISSING_OR_MOVED
    assert verdict.observed_destination_hash is None
    assert not source.exists()


def test_an_occupied_source_path_conflicts_rather_than_overwriting(
        p12_conn, applied):
    plan, source, record, context = applied
    source.write_bytes(b"an unrelated file now lives here")
    verdict = undo(p12_conn, record.journal_entry_id, context=context)
    assert verdict.verdict == v.CONFLICT_SOURCE_PATH_OCCUPIED
    assert source.read_bytes() == b"an unrelated file now lives here"
    assert Path(record.final_destination_path).exists()
    assert verdict.occupant_at_source_hash is not None


def test_an_unavailable_volume_refuses_rather_than_conflicting(
        p12_conn, applied, landscape, ids):
    plan, source, record, context = applied
    import shutil
    shutil.rmtree(landscape["root_documents"] / "Inbox")
    landscape["root_documents"].rename(
        landscape["root_documents"].parent / "Detached")
    verdict = undo(p12_conn, record.journal_entry_id, context=context)
    assert verdict.verdict == v.UNDO_REFUSED_UNAVAILABLE


def test_a_reversal_across_volumes_confirms_at_v4_before_removing(
        p12_conn, planned, landscape, ids):
    plan, source = planned
    other = landscape["root_documents"] / "Coursework"

    def volume_of(path: Path) -> str:
        return "vol-external" if str(path).startswith(str(other)) else "vol-main"

    context = _context(landscape, ids, volume_of=volume_of)
    record = apply_plan(p12_conn, plan, context=context)
    assert record.mode == v.CROSS_VOLUME_COPY_AND_DELETE
    verdict = undo(p12_conn, record.journal_entry_id, context=context)
    assert verdict.verdict == v.REVERSED
    assert source.read_bytes() == b"PHYS1401 syllabus"
    points = [json.loads(row[0])["point"] for row in p12_conn.execute(
        "SELECT explanation FROM events WHERE event_type = 'hashing' "
        "ORDER BY event_id")]
    assert points.count("V4") == 2


def test_undoing_twice_raises_rather_than_reversing_a_reversal(p12_conn, applied):
    plan, _, record, context = applied
    undo(p12_conn, record.journal_entry_id, context=context)
    with pytest.raises(AlreadyReversed):
        undo(p12_conn, record.journal_entry_id, context=context)


def test_undoing_a_reversal_entry_is_refused(p12_conn, applied):
    plan, _, record, context = applied
    verdict = undo(p12_conn, record.journal_entry_id, context=context)
    with pytest.raises(NotAnAppliedEntry):
        undo(p12_conn, verdict.reversal_entry_id, context=context)


def test_undoing_an_unknown_entry_raises(p12_conn, applied):
    _, _, _, context = applied
    with pytest.raises(NoSuchEntry):
        undo(p12_conn, "no-such-entry", context=context)


def test_every_conflict_class_has_its_own_sentence(p12_conn, applied):
    sentences = {v.decline_message(verdict) for verdict in v.UNDO_VERDICTS[1:]}
    assert len(sentences) == len(v.UNDO_VERDICTS) - 1


def test_undo_verdicts_are_readable_back_per_plan(p12_conn, applied):
    plan, _, record, context = applied
    verdict = undo(p12_conn, record.journal_entry_id, context=context)
    assert undo_verdicts_for(p12_conn, plan.plan_id) == (verdict,)
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement `src/mutation/undo.py`**

```python
"""§8.3's conditional undo.

*"Undo must be conditional rather than destructive. ... Before reversing a move,
the system checks that the file at the destination is still the expected content
and that restoring it will not overwrite a newer or unrelated file. If the user
manually edited or moved the file after the product acted, undo should surface a
conflict rather than forcing a rollback."*

A conflict mutates NOTHING and reports the relevant paths and hashes for manual
resolution. That is the whole shape of this module: four questions, any of which
ends the attempt with an answer, and only then a reversal that runs the same
verified discipline as a forward apply.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
import os
import sqlite3
from collections.abc import Mapping
from dataclasses import asdict, dataclass
from pathlib import Path

from database_agent.events import append_event
from database_agent.files_table import HASH_ALGORITHM, observe_path
from database_agent.identity import hash_file
from database_agent.verify import VerificationPoint, verify_content
from evidence_shape.canonical import canonical_json

from mutation.context import ApplyContext
from mutation.cross_volume import DestinationTaken, perform_cross_volume
from mutation.journal import (
    JournalEntry, append_journal_entry, entries_for_plan, entry_by_id, is_reversed,
)
from mutation.plan import MovePlan
from mutation.vocabulary import (
    ATOMIC_RENAME, CONFLICT_DESTINATION_CONTENT_CHANGED,
    CONFLICT_DESTINATION_MISSING_OR_MOVED, CONFLICT_SOURCE_PATH_OCCUPIED,
    CROSS_VOLUME_COPY_AND_DELETE, ENTRY_APPLIED, ENTRY_REVERSAL, REVERSED,
    SUBSYSTEM, UNDO, UNDO_REFUSED_UNAVAILABLE, UNDO_VERDICTS, check,
    decline_message,
)


class NoSuchEntry(KeyError):
    """No journal entry with that id."""


class AlreadyReversed(RuntimeError):
    """A reversal entry already reverses this one. Undo is not idempotent."""


class NotAnAppliedEntry(ValueError):
    """Only an `applied` entry can be undone; a reversal is not a move."""


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class UndoVerdict:
    entry_id: str
    verdict: str
    reversal_entry_id: str | None
    destination_path: str
    original_source_path: str
    expected_hash: str
    observed_destination_hash: str | None
    occupant_at_source_hash: str | None
    directory_outcomes: tuple[tuple[str, str], ...]
    detail: Mapping[str, object]

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        check(self.verdict, UNDO_VERDICTS, name="undo verdict")

    @property
    def reversed_successfully(self) -> bool:
        return self.verdict == REVERSED


def _hash_or_none(path: Path, *, materialized: bool) -> str | None:
    if path.is_symlink() or not path.is_file():
        return None
    try:
        return hash_file(path, materialized=materialized)
    except OSError:
        return None


def _conflict(entry: JournalEntry, verdict: str, *, observed: str | None,
              occupant: str | None, **detail: object) -> UndoVerdict:
    return UndoVerdict(
        entry_id=entry.entry_id, verdict=verdict, reversal_entry_id=None,
        destination_path=entry.destination_path,
        original_source_path=entry.original_source_path,
        expected_hash=entry.content_hash_at_movement,
        observed_destination_hash=observed, occupant_at_source_hash=occupant,
        directory_outcomes=(),
        detail={**detail, "message": decline_message(verdict),
                "destination_path": entry.destination_path,
                "original_source_path": entry.original_source_path,
                "expected_hash": entry.content_hash_at_movement,
                "observed_destination_hash": observed})


def undo(conn: sqlite3.Connection, entry_id: str, *,
         context: ApplyContext) -> UndoVerdict:
    entry = entry_by_id(conn, entry_id)
    if entry is None:
        raise NoSuchEntry(entry_id)
    if entry.entry_kind != ENTRY_APPLIED:
        raise NotAnAppliedEntry(
            f"{entry_id!r} is a {entry.entry_kind} entry; a reversal is not a move")
    if is_reversed(conn, entry_id):
        raise AlreadyReversed(entry_id)

    destination = Path(entry.destination_path)
    source = Path(entry.original_source_path)

    # `66` §11's checks, in order. Availability first: an unmounted volume makes
    # every later answer meaningless, and it is a REFUSAL rather than a conflict
    # because nothing about the file is in doubt.
    for path in (destination.parent, source.parent):
        if not path.exists():
            verdict = _conflict(entry, UNDO_REFUSED_UNAVAILABLE, observed=None,
                                occupant=None, unavailable=str(path))
            _record_undo_verdict(conn, entry, verdict, context=context)
            return verdict

    if not destination.is_file() or destination.is_symlink():
        verdict = _conflict(entry, CONFLICT_DESTINATION_MISSING_OR_MOVED,
                            observed=None, occupant=None)
        _record_undo_verdict(conn, entry, verdict, context=context)
        return verdict

    observed = _hash_or_none(destination, materialized=context.materialized)
    if observed != entry.content_hash_at_movement:
        verdict = _conflict(entry, CONFLICT_DESTINATION_CONTENT_CHANGED,
                            observed=observed, occupant=None)
        _record_undo_verdict(conn, entry, verdict, context=context)
        return verdict

    if os.path.lexists(source):
        verdict = _conflict(
            entry, CONFLICT_SOURCE_PATH_OCCUPIED, observed=observed,
            occupant=_hash_or_none(source, materialized=context.materialized))
        _record_undo_verdict(conn, entry, verdict, context=context)
        return verdict

    # Reversal is itself a mutation and runs the full discipline.
    for point in (VerificationPoint.V1, VerificationPoint.V2):
        verify_content(conn, entry.file_id, entry.content_hash_at_movement,
                       point=point, author=SUBSYSTEM,
                       component_version=context.component_version,
                       materialized=context.materialized)

    mode = (ATOMIC_RENAME
            if context.volume_of(destination) == context.volume_of(source.parent)
            else CROSS_VOLUME_COPY_AND_DELETE)
    if mode == ATOMIC_RENAME:
        try:
            os.link(destination, source)
        except FileExistsError:
            verdict = _conflict(
                entry, CONFLICT_SOURCE_PATH_OCCUPIED, observed=observed,
                occupant=_hash_or_none(source, materialized=context.materialized),
                detected="at the link, between the check and the reversal")
            _record_undo_verdict(conn, entry, verdict, context=context)
            return verdict
        os.unlink(destination)
    else:
        reversal_plan = _reversal_plan(entry)
        try:
            outcome = perform_cross_volume(
                conn, reversal_plan, source=destination, target=source,
                context=context)
        except DestinationTaken:
            verdict = _conflict(
                entry, CONFLICT_SOURCE_PATH_OCCUPIED, observed=observed,
                occupant=_hash_or_none(source, materialized=context.materialized),
                detected="at the exclusive create, mid-reversal")
            _record_undo_verdict(conn, entry, verdict, context=context)
            return verdict
        if not outcome.confirmed:
            verdict = _conflict(
                entry, CONFLICT_DESTINATION_CONTENT_CHANGED, observed=observed,
                occupant=None,
                unverified_reversal_copy=outcome.copy_path,
                note="V4 did not confirm the restored copy; the filed file was "
                     "left exactly where it was (F7)")
            _record_undo_verdict(conn, entry, verdict, context=context)
            return verdict

    stat_result = source.stat()
    observe_path(
        conn, source, author=SUBSYSTEM,
        component_version=context.component_version, filename=source.name,
        normalized_filename=source.name.casefold(), extension=source.suffix,
        observed_size=stat_result.st_size,
        observed_timestamps=str(stat_result.st_mtime),
        parent_folder_context=source.parent.name, mime_type=None,
        detected_format=None, scan_state=context.scan_state,
        materialized=context.materialized)
    v3 = verify_content(
        conn, entry.file_id, entry.content_hash_at_movement,
        point=VerificationPoint.V3, author=SUBSYSTEM,
        component_version=context.component_version,
        materialized=context.materialized)

    reversal_id = context.mint_id()
    at = context.now()
    append_journal_entry(conn, JournalEntry(
        entry_id=reversal_id, entry_kind=ENTRY_REVERSAL,
        reverses_entry_id=entry.entry_id, plan_id=entry.plan_id,
        organization_plan_version=entry.organization_plan_version,
        file_id=entry.file_id, hash_algorithm=HASH_ALGORITHM,
        original_source_path=entry.destination_path,
        destination_path=entry.original_source_path,
        content_hash_at_movement=entry.content_hash_at_movement,
        collision_behaviour=None, post_move_verification_result=v3,
        source_volume=entry.destination_volume,
        destination_volume=entry.source_volume, execution_mode=mode,
        directories_created=(), intended_display_name=entry.intended_display_name,
        filesystem_safe_name=entry.filesystem_safe_name, time_of_execution=at,
        directory_reversal_outcomes=()), record_id=f"{reversal_id}:0")

    verdict = UndoVerdict(
        entry_id=entry.entry_id, verdict=REVERSED, reversal_entry_id=reversal_id,
        destination_path=entry.destination_path,
        original_source_path=entry.original_source_path,
        expected_hash=entry.content_hash_at_movement,
        observed_destination_hash=observed, occupant_at_source_hash=None,
        directory_outcomes=(),
        detail={"execution_mode": mode, "post_move_verification_result": v3})
    _record_undo_verdict(conn, entry, verdict, context=context)
    return verdict


def _reversal_plan(entry: JournalEntry) -> MovePlan:
    """The minimum `perform_cross_volume` reads off a plan: the expected hash, the
    source path and the component version's view of what is being moved. Built
    from the journal entry rather than re-fetched, because the entry is the record
    of what actually happened and the original plan may since have been superseded.
    """
    return MovePlan(
        plan_id=entry.plan_id, file_id=entry.file_id,
        expected_content_hash=entry.content_hash_at_movement,
        expected_source_path=entry.destination_path,
        expected_source_volume=entry.destination_volume,
        expected_size_and_modification_state=canonical_json(
            {"reversal_of": entry.entry_id}),
        requested_destination_node="reversal",
        resolved_destination_path=entry.original_source_path,
        collision_policy="stop_and_ask",
        sensitivity_and_consent_state=canonical_json(
            {"reversal_of": entry.entry_id}),
        reason_and_evidence_summary=canonical_json(
            {"reversal_of": entry.entry_id}),
        required_review_policy="reversal",
        creation_time_and_expiration_state=canonical_json(
            {"created_at": entry.time_of_execution, "expiration_state": "reversal"}),
        organization_plan_version=entry.organization_plan_version,
        placement_decision_reference=entry.entry_id, group_plan_reference=None,
        intended_display_name=entry.intended_display_name,
        filesystem_safe_name=entry.filesystem_safe_name,
        expected_destination_volume=entry.source_volume,
        path_resolution_reference=entry.entry_id,
        destination_root_anchor="reversal", source_high_level_folder=None,
        cross_folder_movement_permission=True)


def _record_undo_verdict(conn: sqlite3.Connection, entry: JournalEntry,
                         verdict: UndoVerdict, *, context: ApplyContext) -> None:
    """One `undo` event per attempt, whatever the verdict.

    A conflict is a result and it is recorded: `66` §9 requires every completed
    action to be visible afterwards, and an undo the person tried and could not
    have is exactly the kind of thing that must not vanish.
    """
    append_event(
        conn, event_type=UNDO, file_id=entry.file_id,
        content_hash=entry.content_hash_at_movement,
        old_path=entry.destination_path, new_path=entry.original_source_path,
        subsystem=SUBSYSTEM, component_version=context.component_version,
        observed_at=context.now(),
        explanation=canonical_json({
            "entry_id": entry.entry_id, "plan_id": entry.plan_id,
            "verdict": verdict.verdict,
            "reversal_entry_id": verdict.reversal_entry_id,
            **dict(verdict.detail)}))


def undo_verdicts_for(conn: sqlite3.Connection,
                      plan_id: str) -> tuple[UndoVerdict, ...]:
    """Every undo attempt on this plan, reconstructed from its `undo` events.

    The verdict has no table of its own: the journal records what happened to the
    file and `events` records what was attempted, and a third home for the same
    fact is the defect class this project has paid for most.
    """
    entries = {entry.entry_id: entry for entry in entries_for_plan(conn, plan_id)}
    verdicts: list[UndoVerdict] = []
    for row in conn.execute(
            "SELECT explanation FROM events WHERE event_type = ? ORDER BY event_id",
            (UNDO,)):
        payload = json.loads(row[0])
        entry = entries.get(payload["entry_id"])
        if entry is None:
            continue
        verdicts.append(UndoVerdict(
            entry_id=payload["entry_id"], verdict=payload["verdict"],
            reversal_entry_id=payload["reversal_entry_id"],
            destination_path=entry.destination_path,
            original_source_path=entry.original_source_path,
            expected_hash=entry.content_hash_at_movement,
            observed_destination_hash=payload.get("observed_destination_hash"),
            occupant_at_source_hash=payload.get("occupant_at_source_hash"),
            directory_outcomes=tuple(
                tuple(pair) for pair in payload.get("directory_outcomes", ())),
            detail={key: value for key, value in payload.items()
                    if key not in {"entry_id", "plan_id", "verdict",
                                   "reversal_entry_id"}}))
    return tuple(verdicts)
```

### Task 12: Reverse the directories this action created, on the same conditional terms

**Files:**
- Create: `src/mutation/directories.py`
- Modify: `src/mutation/undo.py`
- Create: `tests/p12/test_p12_directory_reversal.py`

**Interfaces:**

*Consumes:* `mutation.journal.{JournalEntry, applied_entries}`, `mutation.vocabulary.DIRECTORY_REVERSAL_OUTCOMES`.

*Produces:*

```python
def reverse_directories(conn, entry: JournalEntry
                        ) -> tuple[tuple[str, str], ...]: ...
```

and in `undo.py`, on the successful-reversal path only:

```python
    directory_outcomes = reverse_directories(conn, entry)
```
threaded into both the reversal `JournalEntry`'s `directory_reversal_outcomes` and the `UndoVerdict`'s `directory_outcomes`.

**Done-means:** **20** in full — *"Directories created by an action appear on both the execution record and the journal entry. Undo removes one only when it is still empty and unreferenced by another applied entry; a directory that has since received another file is retained with the reason recorded, and the file reversal still reports `reversed`."*

> **The three conditions are conjunctive and the order is deepest-first.** SPEC Contract out §7: a created directory is removed **only** when all three hold — this journal entry recorded creating it, it is still empty, and no other journal entry that is **still applied** moved a file into it or beneath it. Removal proceeds deepest-first through the chain this entry created and **stops at the first directory that fails a condition**, because a directory whose child survived is not empty and evaluating it further would be asking a question whose answer is already known.

> **A retained directory is never a conflict.** The file reversal succeeded; a folder that stayed behind is a fact about the folder, not a failure of the undo. The verdict stays `reversed` and the retention and its reason go on the reversal entry.

> **Nothing here can remove a user file.** §7.11's prohibition is untouched: an empty directory contains none, and a directory P12 did not create is never a candidate. `os.rmdir` is used rather than any recursive removal — it refuses a non-empty directory at the system-call level, so "still empty" is guaranteed by the kernel and not only by the check above it.

> **Why `applied_entries` and not every entry.** An entry whose own move has since been undone must not hold a directory hostage: its file is no longer there. `journal.applied_entries` is defined as *applied entries no reversal entry has reversed*, which is exactly that question.

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement `src/mutation/directories.py`**

```python
"""Contract out §7 — reversing the directories one action created.

§8.3 requires every mutation to be reversible, and creating a directory for a
frozen node is a mutation P12 performed. On undo, a created directory is removed
ONLY when all three hold: this journal entry recorded creating it, it is still
empty, and no other journal entry that is STILL APPLIED moved a file into it or
beneath it. Removal proceeds deepest-first and stops at the first directory that
fails a condition. Otherwise the directory is RETAINED and the retention and its
reason are recorded on the reversal entry -- a retained directory is never a
conflict, because the file reversal itself succeeded.

`os.rmdir` and nothing else. It refuses a non-empty directory at the system-call
level, so "still empty" is guaranteed by the kernel rather than only by the check
above it, and there is no code path here through which a recursive removal could
be reached.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import os
import sqlite3
from pathlib import Path

from mutation.journal import JournalEntry, applied_entries
from mutation.vocabulary import (
    DIR_REMOVED, DIR_RETAINED_NOT_CREATED, DIR_RETAINED_NOT_EMPTY,
    DIR_RETAINED_REFERENCED,
)


def _referenced_by_another_applied_entry(conn: sqlite3.Connection,
                                         directory: Path,
                                         entry: JournalEntry) -> bool:
    """Did some other entry that is still applied put a file in or beneath this?

    Reversed entries are excluded by `applied_entries`: an entry whose own move
    has been undone has no file there any more and must not hold a directory.
    """
    for other in applied_entries(conn):
        if other.entry_id == entry.entry_id:
            continue
        try:
            Path(other.destination_path).relative_to(directory)
        except ValueError:
            continue
        return True
    return False


def reverse_directories(conn: sqlite3.Connection,
                        entry: JournalEntry) -> tuple[tuple[str, str], ...]:
    """Deepest-first, conjunctive, stopping at the first failure."""
    outcomes: list[tuple[str, str]] = []
    stopped = False
    for path in reversed(entry.directories_created):
        directory = Path(path)
        if not directory.is_dir():
            outcomes.append((path, DIR_RETAINED_NOT_CREATED))
            stopped = True
            break
        if _referenced_by_another_applied_entry(conn, directory, entry):
            outcomes.append((path, DIR_RETAINED_REFERENCED))
            stopped = True
            break
        try:
            os.rmdir(directory)
        except OSError:
            # Non-empty, or a permission the product no longer has. Either way the
            # directory stays and nothing inside it is touched.
            outcomes.append((path, DIR_RETAINED_NOT_EMPTY))
            stopped = True
            break
        outcomes.append((path, DIR_REMOVED))

    if not stopped and entry.directories_created:
        # The chain was removed all the way up to a directory P12 did not create.
        # Reporting the boundary is what makes the stopping point legible instead
        # of the report simply ending.
        boundary = Path(entry.directories_created[0]).parent
        outcomes.append((str(boundary), DIR_RETAINED_NOT_CREATED))
    return tuple(outcomes)
```

### Task 13: `66` §11's undo retention, §8.6's run legibility, and `66` §9's activity list

> **This task is not in the SPEC's "Done means".** It exists because **A1** makes `66` govern user-facing behaviour and `66` §11, §9 and §10 state three things the SPEC does not: a default undo retention period with user-selectable alternatives, an activity list with named columns, and distinct refusal messages. Build it; it is the difference between a correct transaction engine and a product a person can trust.

> **UNRESOLVED — F9 (narrowed) and F16.** SPEC Open question 9 asks whether elapsed time or a new plan version ends the ability to undo. `66` §11 answers the **time** half — *"The recommended default undo retention period is 90 days. The user should be able to select 30 days, 90 days, one year, or retention until manually cleared, subject to local storage limits that are clearly explained."* — and says nothing about the plan-version half, which stays open. **F16, a judgement:** past its retention period P12 stops **offering** undo (`undo_available` is `False`) and does **not** make a late `undo()` call fail, because `66` says *"expose conditional undo until the stated expiration"* and no sentence anywhere says a late reversal must be refused. The journal itself is never purged: *"The product should never silently purge active-policy history in a way that makes a recent move impossible to understand or review."*

> **UNRESOLVED — F17. `66` §9's activity list wants "the policy that authorized it", and no filing policy exists.** The filing-policy layer is item 5 of `66` §22's release order, after P12. `ActivityRow.authorizing_policy` therefore reports the plan's `Required review policy` and the row records that the filing policy is absent, rather than leaving the column blank or inventing a policy id. **F10 is the same gap seen from the retention side**: `66` §8 makes "Undo period" a per-policy dimension and P12 owns only a corpus-wide setting.

```python
"""`66` §11's retention promise. 90 days by default, four user choices, and a
journal that is never purged."""
from __future__ import annotations

import sqlite3

import pytest

from mutation import vocabulary as v
from mutation.journal import JournalEntry, append_journal_entry
from mutation.retention import (
    DEFAULT_UNDO_RETENTION, RETENTION_DAYS, UNDO_RETENTION_CHOICES,
    UnknownRetentionChoice, current_undo_retention, set_undo_retention,
    undo_available,
)


def _entry(at="2026-05-01T00:00:00Z"):
    return JournalEntry(
        entry_id="j-1", entry_kind=v.ENTRY_APPLIED, reverses_entry_id=None,
        plan_id="p-1", organization_plan_version="plan-1", file_id="f-1",
        hash_algorithm="sha256", original_source_path="/a/x.pdf",
        destination_path="/b/x.pdf", content_hash_at_movement="h1",
        collision_behaviour=None, post_move_verification_result="match",
        source_volume="v", destination_volume="v",
        execution_mode=v.ATOMIC_RENAME, directories_created=(),
        intended_display_name="x.pdf", filesystem_safe_name="x.pdf",
        time_of_execution=at, directory_reversal_outcomes=())


def test_the_four_choices_and_the_recommended_default():
    assert UNDO_RETENTION_CHOICES == (
        "30_days", "90_days", "365_days", "until_cleared")
    assert DEFAULT_UNDO_RETENTION == "90_days"
    assert RETENTION_DAYS == {"30_days": 30, "90_days": 90, "365_days": 365,
                              "until_cleared": None}


def test_an_unset_database_reports_the_recommended_default(p12_conn):
    assert current_undo_retention(p12_conn) == DEFAULT_UNDO_RETENTION


def test_the_user_can_select_each_of_the_four(p12_conn):
    for index, choice in enumerate(UNDO_RETENTION_CHOICES):
        set_undo_retention(p12_conn, choice, user_id="user-1",
                           now=f"2026-08-29T0{index}:00:00Z",
                           record_id=f"ret-{index}")
        assert current_undo_retention(p12_conn) == choice


def test_a_changed_setting_supersedes_and_the_old_row_is_retained(p12_conn):
    set_undo_retention(p12_conn, "30_days", user_id="user-1",
                       now="2026-08-29T00:00:00Z", record_id="ret-0")
    set_undo_retention(p12_conn, "365_days", user_id="user-1",
                       now="2026-08-29T01:00:00Z", record_id="ret-1")
    rows = p12_conn.execute(
        "SELECT retention_choice, superseded_by FROM undo_retention "
        "ORDER BY set_at").fetchall()
    assert [row[0] for row in rows] == ["30_days", "365_days"]
    assert rows[0][1] is not None and rows[1][1] is None


def test_an_unknown_choice_is_refused(p12_conn):
    with pytest.raises(UnknownRetentionChoice):
        set_undo_retention(p12_conn, "forever_and_ever", user_id="user-1",
                           now="2026-08-29T00:00:00Z", record_id="ret-x")


def test_undo_is_offered_inside_the_period_and_not_outside_it():
    entry = _entry(at="2026-05-01T00:00:00Z")
    assert undo_available(entry, retention="365_days",
                          now="2026-08-29T00:00:00Z") is True
    assert undo_available(entry, retention="90_days",
                          now="2026-08-29T00:00:00Z") is False
    assert undo_available(entry, retention="30_days",
                          now="2026-05-15T00:00:00Z") is True


def test_until_cleared_never_expires():
    assert undo_available(_entry(at="2019-01-01T00:00:00Z"),
                          retention="until_cleared",
                          now="2026-08-29T00:00:00Z") is True


def test_the_boundary_day_is_still_inside_the_period():
    entry = _entry(at="2026-05-31T00:00:00Z")
    assert undo_available(entry, retention="90_days",
                          now="2026-08-29T00:00:00Z") is True


def test_the_journal_is_never_purged_by_a_shorter_retention(p12_conn):
    append_journal_entry(p12_conn, _entry(at="2019-01-01T00:00:00Z"),
                         record_id="rec-1")
    set_undo_retention(p12_conn, "30_days", user_id="user-1",
                       now="2026-08-29T00:00:00Z", record_id="ret-0")
    assert p12_conn.execute("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM move_journal").fetchone()[0] == 1
    with pytest.raises(sqlite3.IntegrityError):
        p12_conn.execute("DELETE FROM move_journal")
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Implement `src/mutation/retention.py`**

```python
"""`66` §11's undo retention. The visible period, not a purge.

*"The recommended default undo retention period is 90 days. The user should be
able to select 30 days, 90 days, one year, or retention until manually cleared,
subject to local storage limits that are clearly explained. The product should
never silently purge active-policy history in a way that makes a recent move
impossible to understand or review."*

Two things follow and both matter. The journal is NEVER deleted -- its table
refuses a delete by trigger, so retention cannot become a purge even by accident.
And retention decides whether undo is OFFERED, not whether a reversal that is
asked for is refused: `66` says *"expose conditional undo until the stated
expiration"*, and no sentence anywhere says a late undo must fail (F16).
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import sqlite3
from collections.abc import Mapping
from datetime import datetime, timedelta

from database_agent.supersede import mark_superseded
from evidence_shape.canonical import canonical_json

from mutation.journal import JournalEntry

UNDO_RETENTION_CHOICES: tuple[str, ...] = (
    "30_days", "90_days", "365_days", "until_cleared")

#: `66` §11's word is "recommended", and this is the recommendation. The user
#: changes it; P12 never changes it for them.
DEFAULT_UNDO_RETENTION: str = "90_days"

RETENTION_DAYS: Mapping[str, int | None] = {
    "30_days": 30, "90_days": 90, "365_days": 365, "until_cleared": None}


class UnknownRetentionChoice(ValueError):
    """A period outside `66` §11's four. There is no fifth."""


def _parse(moment: str) -> datetime:
    return datetime.fromisoformat(moment.replace("Z", "+00:00"))


def set_undo_retention(conn: sqlite3.Connection, choice: str, *,
                       user_id: str | None, now: str, record_id: str) -> str:
    if choice not in UNDO_RETENTION_CHOICES:
        raise UnknownRetentionChoice(
            f"{choice!r} is not one of {UNDO_RETENTION_CHOICES}")
    previous = conn.execute(
        "SELECT record_id FROM undo_retention WHERE superseded_by IS NULL"
    ).fetchone()
    conn.execute(
        "INSERT INTO undo_retention (record_id, retention_choice, set_at, set_by, "
        "payload) VALUES (?,?,?,?,?)",
        (record_id, choice, now, user_id,
         canonical_json({"retention_choice": choice, "set_at": now,
                         "set_by": user_id,
                         "days": RETENTION_DAYS[choice]})))
    if previous is not None:
        mark_superseded(conn, "undo_retention", old_id=previous[0],
                        new_id=record_id, reason="the user changed the period")
    return record_id


def current_undo_retention(conn: sqlite3.Connection) -> str:
    row = conn.execute(
        "SELECT retention_choice FROM undo_retention WHERE superseded_by IS NULL"
    ).fetchone()
    return DEFAULT_UNDO_RETENTION if row is None else row[0]


def undo_available(entry: JournalEntry, *, retention: str, now: str) -> bool:
    """Is undo still OFFERED for this entry? Not: would a reversal be refused."""
    if retention not in UNDO_RETENTION_CHOICES:
        raise UnknownRetentionChoice(
            f"{retention!r} is not one of {UNDO_RETENTION_CHOICES}")
    days = RETENTION_DAYS[retention]
    if days is None:
        return True
    return _parse(now) <= _parse(entry.time_of_execution) + timedelta(days=days)
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Implement `src/mutation/report.py`**

```python
"""§8.6's run legibility and `66` §9's activity list.

§8.6, in P12's terms: *"an apply run reports applied / refused / stale / paused /
failed counts, so deferred work is visible rather than presented as completed."*
The five counts are separate fields and `total` is their sum, so there is no
arithmetic under which a stale plan can be reported as a move that happened.

`66` §9: *"Every completed action appears in a reviewable activity list with the
source path, destination path, evidence summary, policy that authorized it,
collision behavior, move time, current status, and undo availability."* Every one
of those columns is below. `authorizing_policy` reports the plan's REVIEW policy
and `filing_policy_present` says plainly that the filing policy `66` §9 means does
not exist yet (F17) -- a blank column would read as "no policy authorized this",
which is a different and worse claim.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
import sqlite3
from collections.abc import Mapping, Sequence
from dataclasses import dataclass

from mutation.execute import execution_records_for
from mutation.journal import entry_by_id
from mutation.plan import MovePlan, current_plan
from mutation.retention import undo_available
from mutation.vocabulary import (
    APPLIED, FAILED, PAUSED, REFUSED, STALE, decline_message,
)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ApplyRunReport:
    applied: int
    refused: int
    stale: int
    paused: int
    failed: int
    refusals_by_class: Mapping[str, int]
    stale_by_trigger: Mapping[str, int]
    paused_by_reason: Mapping[str, int]
    failed_by_class: Mapping[str, int]
    declines: tuple[tuple[str, str], ...]

    @property
    def total(self) -> int:
        return self.applied + self.refused + self.stale + self.paused + self.failed


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ActivityRow:
    plan_id: str
    source_path: str
    destination_path: str
    reason_and_evidence_summary: str
    authorizing_policy: str
    filing_policy_present: bool
    collision_behaviour: str | None
    moved_at: str
    status: str
    undo_available: bool
    journal_entry_id: str | None


def _split(result: str) -> tuple[str, str | None]:
    head, _, tail = result.partition(":")
    return head, tail or None


def apply_report(conn: sqlite3.Connection, *,
                 plan_ids: Sequence[str]) -> ApplyRunReport:
    counts = {APPLIED: 0, REFUSED: 0, STALE: 0, PAUSED: 0, FAILED: 0}
    by_class: dict[str, dict[str, int]] = {
        REFUSED: {}, STALE: {}, PAUSED: {}, FAILED: {}}
    declines: list[tuple[str, str]] = []
    for plan_id in plan_ids:
        records = execution_records_for(conn, plan_id)
        if not records:
            continue
        # The LAST record for a plan is its current outcome: a re-attempt after a
        # refresh appends, and an earlier refusal is history rather than a second
        # thing that happened to the file.
        result = records[-1].result
        head, tail = _split(result)
        counts[head] += 1
        if tail is not None:
            by_class[head][tail] = by_class[head].get(tail, 0) + 1
        if head != APPLIED:
            declines.append((plan_id, decline_message(
                result if head != REFUSED else tail)))
    return ApplyRunReport(
        applied=counts[APPLIED], refused=counts[REFUSED], stale=counts[STALE],
        paused=counts[PAUSED], failed=counts[FAILED],
        refusals_by_class=dict(by_class[REFUSED]),
        stale_by_trigger=dict(by_class[STALE]),
        paused_by_reason=dict(by_class[PAUSED]),
        failed_by_class=dict(by_class[FAILED]), declines=tuple(declines))


def activity(conn: sqlite3.Connection, *, retention: str,
             now: str) -> tuple[ActivityRow, ...]:
    rows: list[ActivityRow] = []
    for plan_id, in conn.execute(
            "SELECT DISTINCT plan_id FROM execution_records ORDER BY plan_id"):
        plan = current_plan(conn, plan_id)
        if plan is None:
            continue
        record = execution_records_for(conn, plan_id)[-1]
        entry = (None if record.journal_entry_id is None
                 else entry_by_id(conn, record.journal_entry_id))
        rows.append(ActivityRow(
            plan_id=plan_id, source_path=plan.expected_source_path,
            destination_path=(record.final_destination_path
                              or plan.resolved_destination_path),
            reason_and_evidence_summary=plan.reason_and_evidence_summary,
            authorizing_policy=plan.required_review_policy,
            filing_policy_present=False,
            collision_behaviour=(entry.collision_behaviour
                                 if entry is not None else None),
            moved_at=record.finished_at, status=record.result,
            undo_available=(entry is not None
                            and undo_available(entry, retention=retention,
                                               now=now)),
            journal_entry_id=record.journal_entry_id))
    return tuple(rows)
```

### Task 14: The walking skeleton, the published fixtures, the replay run, and the guards

> **UNRESOLVED — F3. P2's `STAGE_IDS` carries no mutation stage, so P12 emits no stage-output envelope.** The ten are `extraction`, `factual_validation`, `retrieval`, `graph_construction`, `llm_interpretation`, `grouping`, `template_generation`, `tree_design`, `candidate_node_retrieval`, `placement_scoring`. `eval_harness.stage_output.record_stage_output` calls `check_stage`, which raises `UnknownStage` for anything else, and adding a name edits a P2 file, which **A5** forbids. **`src/mutation/stage_output.py` is therefore deliberately not created.** Done-means 15's substance — *"Plan construction and precondition evaluation run under P2's replay against a fixture root without touching a live filesystem"* — is satisfied by driving them inside a real P2 run (`start_run` / `finish_run`) and proving no mutation occurred; the envelope half needs a P2 SPEC edit that is not P12's to make.

> **UNRESOLVED — F6. The batch bound and the halt rule.** §8.3 permits applying *"one action at a time or in a safely bounded batch"* and states no size; only a sync conflict is named as pause-worthy. This task implements **exactly** what the design states — a pause halts the remainder of the run, every other outcome continues to the next plan — and takes `batch_limit: int | None` as a required keyword so the bound is the caller's and never P12's. Whether any other refusal or failure should halt a batch is open.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing guard tests**

Create `tests/p12/test_p12_guards.py`:

```python
"""P12's structural guarantees, asserted by introspection rather than by grep.

A source-text search matches comments and docstrings, and scanning text for a
token has produced a false result nine times on this project. Every guard here
either parses the AST or reads the imported module object.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import ast
import importlib
import pathlib
import pkgutil
import sys
from pathlib import Path

import pytest

SOURCE = pathlib.Path("src/mutation")

#: Everything `src/mutation/` may import at top level. Standard library plus the
#: first-party packages P12 legitimately consumes. Any name outside this set in a
#: P12 module is a dependency nobody approved.
PERMITTED_TOP_LEVEL = frozenset({
    # stdlib
    "__future__", "ast", "collections", "contextlib", "dataclasses", "datetime",
    "enum", "json", "os", "pathlib", "shutil", "sqlite3", "types", "typing",
    "unicodedata", "uuid",
    # first-party
    "database_agent", "evidence_shape", "placement", "privacy", "scan_agent",
    "tree_design", "mutation",
})


def _modules():
    for path in sorted(SOURCE.glob("*.py")):
        yield path, ast.parse(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))


def _imported_names(tree):
    for node in ast.walk(tree):
        if isinstance(node, ast.Import):
            for alias in node.names:
                yield alias.name.split(".")[0]
        elif isinstance(node, ast.ImportFrom) and node.level == 0 and node.module:
            yield node.module.split(".")[0]


def test_p12_imports_only_the_standard_library_and_approved_first_party():
    for path, tree in _modules():
        for name in _imported_names(tree):
            assert name in PERMITTED_TOP_LEVEL, f"{path} imports {name!r}"


def test_p12_imports_no_test_module():
    for path, tree in _modules():
        for name in _imported_names(tree):
            assert not name.startswith("tests"), f"{path} imports {name!r}"


def test_p12_imports_no_llm_or_reader_package():
    """P12 reads no content and calls no model. It moves bytes it never inspects."""
    for path, tree in _modules():
        for name in _imported_names(tree):
            assert name not in {"llm_harness", "readers", "facts", "grouping",
                                "recognition", "eval_harness"}, f"{path}: {name}"


def _calls_named(tree, names):
    for node in ast.walk(tree):
        if isinstance(node, ast.Call):
            func = node.func
            if isinstance(func, ast.Attribute) and func.attr in names:
                yield func.attr
            elif isinstance(func, ast.Name) and func.id in names:
                yield func.id


def test_no_recursive_or_bulk_removal_exists_anywhere_in_p12():
    """§7.11. `rmtree` and `removedirs` can remove a user file; neither is here."""
    for path, tree in _modules():
        found = set(_calls_named(tree, {"rmtree", "removedirs", "unlink_all"}))
        assert not found, f"{path} calls {sorted(found)}"


def test_every_file_removal_lives_in_one_of_three_modules():
    """`os.unlink` is the only removal primitive P12 uses, and only three modules
    may call it: the same-volume move (after `os.link` made the bytes reachable at
    two names), the cross-volume move (after V4 confirmed them), and the reversal,
    which is a move. `directories.py` uses `os.rmdir`, which cannot remove a file.
    """
    allowed = {"execute.py", "cross_volume.py", "undo.py"}
    for path, tree in _modules():
        if set(_calls_named(tree, {"unlink", "remove"})) and path.name not in allowed:
            pytest.fail(f"{path} removes a path and is not one of {sorted(allowed)}")


def test_directory_removal_uses_rmdir_which_cannot_remove_a_file():
    tree = ast.parse((SOURCE / "directories.py").read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    assert "rmdir" in set(_calls_named(tree, {"rmdir"}))
    assert not set(_calls_named(tree, {"unlink", "remove", "rmtree"}))


def test_no_module_level_constant_holds_a_path_a_threshold_or_a_suffix_format():
    """A7. Every threshold, constraint table, suffix format and root path is
    injected. A module-level number or path in P12 would be an answer the design
    did not give -- with two named exceptions, both quoted from a design document.
    """
    exceptions = {
        # `66` §11's four periods, in days. The design states them literally.
        ("mutation.retention", "RETENTION_DAYS"),
    }
    sys.path.insert(0, "src")
    for path, _ in _modules():
        if path.name == "__init__.py":
            continue
        module = importlib.import_module(f"mutation.{path.stem}")
        for name, value in vars(module).items():
            if name.startswith("_") or (module.__name__, name) in exceptions:
                continue
            assert not isinstance(value, Path), f"{module.__name__}.{name} is a Path"
            if isinstance(value, (int, float)) and not isinstance(value, bool):
                pytest.fail(f"{module.__name__}.{name} is a bare number")
            if isinstance(value, dict) and any(
                    isinstance(item, (int, float)) and not isinstance(item, bool)
                    for item in value.values()):
                pytest.fail(f"{module.__name__}.{name} holds numbers")


def test_every_closed_vocabulary_is_bound_in_exactly_one_module():
    """A6. `vocabulary` publishes each closed set; no other module rebinds one."""
    sys.path.insert(0, "src")
    from mutation import vocabulary as v

    closed = {
        "STALENESS_TRIGGERS": v.STALENESS_TRIGGERS,
        "REFUSAL_CLASSES": v.REFUSAL_CLASSES,
        "COLLISION_BEHAVIOURS": v.COLLISION_BEHAVIOURS,
        "COLLISION_KINDS": v.COLLISION_KINDS,
        "EXECUTION_MODES": v.EXECUTION_MODES,
        "RESULT_KINDS": v.RESULT_KINDS,
        "UNDO_VERDICTS": v.UNDO_VERDICTS,
        "DIRECTORY_REVERSAL_OUTCOMES": v.DIRECTORY_REVERSAL_OUTCOMES,
        "CROSS_FOLDER_VERDICTS": v.CROSS_FOLDER_VERDICTS,
        "NORMALIZATIONS": v.NORMALIZATIONS,
        "PAUSE_REASONS": v.PAUSE_REASONS,
    }
    for path, _ in _modules():
        if path.name in {"__init__.py", "vocabulary.py"}:
            continue
        module = importlib.import_module(f"mutation.{path.stem}")
        for name, value in vars(module).items():
            if not isinstance(value, tuple) or name.startswith("_"):
                continue
            for label, members in closed.items():
                assert set(value) != set(members) or not members, (
                    f"{module.__name__}.{name} is a second home for {label}")


def test_p12_publishes_no_sensitive_class_set():
    """C5 is open -- *is `protected` exactly the top two handling classes?* --
    and publishing the set would answer it in code."""
    sys.path.insert(0, "src")
    for path, _ in _modules():
        if path.name == "__init__.py":
            continue
        module = importlib.import_module(f"mutation.{path.stem}")
        for name in vars(module):
            assert "SENSITIVE_CLASSES" not in name
            assert "PROTECTED_CLASSES" not in name


def test_p12_registers_no_new_event_type():
    sys.path.insert(0, "src")
    from database_agent.events import RESERVED_EVENT_TYPES
    from mutation import vocabulary as v
    assert set(v.EVENT_TYPES) <= RESERVED_EVENT_TYPES
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Implement `src/mutation/pipeline.py`**

```python
"""The sequencer. One plan at a time, in order, with the run's counts at the end.

§8.3 permits *"one action at a time or in a safely bounded batch"* and states no
size, so `batch_limit` is a required keyword the caller supplies -- the bound is
theirs. The one halt rule the design states is that a sync conflict pauses the
run; every other outcome continues to the next plan and the run's report carries
what happened to each. Whether any other refusal or failure should halt the
remainder is SPEC Open question 4 and is not answered here (F6).

A `refresh_required` approval routes to the staleness path: the plan is rebuilt
from the decision, supersedes the old one, and is attempted once. Once, and not in
a loop -- a second refresh request means the user is asking again, and asking
again is a new run.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import sqlite3
from collections.abc import Callable, Sequence
from dataclasses import dataclass

from placement.records import PlacementDecision

from mutation.approval import ReviewApproval, refresh_requested
from mutation.context import ApplyContext
from mutation.execute import ExecutionRecord, apply_plan
from mutation.plan import PlanRefused, build_plan, record_plan, supersede_plan
from mutation.report import ApplyRunReport, apply_report
from mutation.vocabulary import PAUSED


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class RunOutcome:
    attempted: tuple[ExecutionRecord, ...]
    not_attempted: tuple[str, ...]
    no_plan: tuple[str, ...]
    refused_to_plan: tuple[tuple[str, str], ...]
    report: ApplyRunReport


def run_apply(conn: sqlite3.Connection,
              decisions: Sequence[PlacementDecision], *,
              context: ApplyContext, collision_policy: str,
              expiration_state: str, cross_folder_moves: bool,
              batch_limit: int | None,
              approval_for: Callable[[str], ReviewApproval | None]) -> RunOutcome:
    selected = list(decisions if batch_limit is None else decisions[:batch_limit])
    attempted: list[ExecutionRecord] = []
    not_attempted: list[str] = []
    no_plan: list[str] = []
    refused_to_plan: list[tuple[str, str]] = []
    plan_ids: list[str] = []
    halted = False

    for index, decision in enumerate(selected):
        if halted:
            not_attempted.append(decision.decision_id)
            continue
        try:
            built = build_plan(
                conn, decision, nodes=context.nodes,
                legal_destination_ids=context.legal_destination_ids,
                cross_folder_moves=cross_folder_moves,
                constraints=context.constraints,
                high_level_folders=context.high_level_folders,
                volume_of=context.volume_of, collision_policy=collision_policy,
                expiration_state=expiration_state, now=context.now,
                mint_id=context.mint_id)
        except PlanRefused as refused:
            refused_to_plan.append((decision.decision_id, refused.refusal_class))
            continue
        if built is None:
            # Not a refusal: P11's other six outcomes produce no plan at all.
            no_plan.append(decision.decision_id)
            continue

        plan, resolution = built
        record_plan(conn, plan, resolution, created_at=context.now(),
                    component_version=context.component_version)
        plan_ids.append(plan.plan_id)
        record = apply_plan(conn, plan, context=context)

        if (record.result.startswith(f"{PAUSED}:")
                is False and refresh_requested(plan, approval_for=approval_for)):
            rebuilt = build_plan(
                conn, decision, nodes=context.nodes,
                legal_destination_ids=context.legal_destination_ids,
                cross_folder_moves=cross_folder_moves,
                constraints=context.constraints,
                high_level_folders=context.high_level_folders,
                volume_of=context.volume_of, collision_policy=collision_policy,
                expiration_state=expiration_state, now=context.now,
                mint_id=context.mint_id)
            if rebuilt is not None:
                fresh_plan, fresh_resolution = rebuilt
                supersede_plan(
                    conn, plan.plan_id, fresh_plan, fresh_resolution,
                    reason="the user asked for the plan to be refreshed",
                    created_at=context.now(),
                    component_version=context.component_version)
                plan_ids.append(fresh_plan.plan_id)
                record = apply_plan(conn, fresh_plan, context=context)

        attempted.append(record)
        if record.result.startswith(f"{PAUSED}:"):
            halted = True

    return RunOutcome(
        attempted=tuple(attempted), not_attempted=tuple(not_attempted),
        no_plan=tuple(no_plan), refused_to_plan=tuple(refused_to_plan),
        report=apply_report(conn, plan_ids=plan_ids))
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Implement `src/mutation/fixtures.py`**

```python
"""The fixtures P12 publishes so neighbours can build before it exists.

SPEC, Contract in: one plan-record fixture per outcome class; one journal-entry
fixture and one undo-verdict fixture per undo outcome; a fixture directory holding
§8.3's hazards; and three resolution shapes P10 and P11 need in order to see what
a `root_anchor` and label chain must supply.

`hazard_root` builds real files and directories, because a hazard that is only a
string proves nothing: a symlink has to be a symlink and an NFC/NFD pair has to be
two entries whose bytes differ. It takes the root it may write into and creates
nothing outside it.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Mapping
from pathlib import Path

from tree_design.records import Node

from mutation.journal import JournalEntry
from mutation.plan import MovePlan
from mutation.undo import UndoVerdict
from mutation.vocabulary import (
    APPLIED, ATOMIC_RENAME, AWAITING_COLLISION_DECISION,
    CONFLICT_DESTINATION_CONTENT_CHANGED, CONFLICT_DESTINATION_MISSING_OR_MOVED,
    CONFLICT_SOURCE_PATH_OCCUPIED, CONTENT_HASH_DIFFERS, ENTRY_APPLIED, FAILED,
    PAUSED, PRESERVE_BOTH_DETERMINISTIC_SUFFIX, REFUSED, REVERSED, STALE,
    SUFFIXED_PATH, UNDO_REFUSED_UNAVAILABLE, V3_HASH_MISMATCH,
    REVIEW_POLICY_UNSATISFIED, decline_message,
)

T0 = "2026-08-29T00:00:00Z"
FIXTURE_HASH = "a" * 64
_SOURCE = "/fixture/Documents/Inbox/Syllabus.pdf"
_DESTINATION = "/fixture/Documents/Coursework/PHYS1401/Syllabus.pdf"


def _plan(plan_id: str, *, collision_policy: str = PRESERVE_BOTH_DETERMINISTIC_SUFFIX,
          review_policy: str = "auto_eligible") -> MovePlan:
    return MovePlan(
        plan_id=plan_id, file_id="f-syllabus", expected_content_hash=FIXTURE_HASH,
        expected_source_path=_SOURCE, expected_source_volume="vol-main",
        expected_size_and_modification_state=
            '{"observed_size":17,"observed_timestamps":"1756000000.0"}',
        requested_destination_node="n-phys",
        resolved_destination_path=_DESTINATION, collision_policy=collision_policy,
        sensitivity_and_consent_state=
            '{"consent_audit_ref":null,"handling_class":"personal_non_sensitive",'
            '"model_eligibility":"dossier_permitted","protected":false}',
        reason_and_evidence_summary=
            '{"evidence_type":"direct","explanation":"subject = PHYS1401"}',
        required_review_policy=review_policy,
        creation_time_and_expiration_state=
            '{"created_at":"2026-08-29T00:00:00Z","expiration_state":"none"}',
        organization_plan_version="plan-1",
        placement_decision_reference="d-syllabus", group_plan_reference=None,
        intended_display_name="Syllabus.pdf", filesystem_safe_name="Syllabus.pdf",
        expected_destination_volume="vol-main",
        path_resolution_reference="res-1", destination_root_anchor="root_documents",
        source_high_level_folder="root_documents",
        cross_folder_movement_permission=True)


#: One plan-record fixture per outcome class the SPEC names.
PLAN_FIXTURES: Mapping[str, MovePlan] = {
    APPLIED: _plan("plan-applied"),
    f"{STALE}:{CONTENT_HASH_DIFFERS}": _plan("plan-stale"),
    SUFFIXED_PATH: _plan("plan-suffixed"),
    f"{REFUSED}:{REVIEW_POLICY_UNSATISFIED}": _plan(
        "plan-refused", review_policy="review_required"),
    f"{PAUSED}:{AWAITING_COLLISION_DECISION}": _plan(
        "plan-paused", collision_policy="stop_and_ask"),
    f"{FAILED}:{V3_HASH_MISMATCH}": _plan("plan-failed"),
}


def plan_fixture(outcome: str) -> MovePlan:
    return PLAN_FIXTURES[outcome]


def journal_entry_fixture() -> JournalEntry:
    return JournalEntry(
        entry_id="j-fixture", entry_kind=ENTRY_APPLIED, reverses_entry_id=None,
        plan_id="plan-applied", organization_plan_version="plan-1",
        file_id="f-syllabus", hash_algorithm="sha256",
        original_source_path=_SOURCE, destination_path=_DESTINATION,
        content_hash_at_movement=FIXTURE_HASH, collision_behaviour=None,
        post_move_verification_result="match", source_volume="vol-main",
        destination_volume="vol-main", execution_mode=ATOMIC_RENAME,
        directories_created=("/fixture/Documents/Coursework",
                             "/fixture/Documents/Coursework/PHYS1401"),
        intended_display_name="Syllabus.pdf", filesystem_safe_name="Syllabus.pdf",
        time_of_execution=T0, directory_reversal_outcomes=())


def _verdict(verdict: str, *, observed: str | None, occupant: str | None,
             reversal: str | None) -> UndoVerdict:
    return UndoVerdict(
        entry_id="j-fixture", verdict=verdict, reversal_entry_id=reversal,
        destination_path=_DESTINATION, original_source_path=_SOURCE,
        expected_hash=FIXTURE_HASH, observed_destination_hash=observed,
        occupant_at_source_hash=occupant, directory_outcomes=(),
        detail={"message": decline_message(verdict)} if verdict != REVERSED else {})


def undo_verdict_fixtures() -> Mapping[str, UndoVerdict]:
    """One per undo outcome: reversed, and each conflict class."""
    return {
        REVERSED: UndoVerdict(
            entry_id="j-fixture", verdict=REVERSED, reversal_entry_id="j-reversal",
            destination_path=_DESTINATION, original_source_path=_SOURCE,
            expected_hash=FIXTURE_HASH, observed_destination_hash=FIXTURE_HASH,
            occupant_at_source_hash=None, directory_outcomes=(),
            detail={"execution_mode": ATOMIC_RENAME}),
        CONFLICT_DESTINATION_CONTENT_CHANGED: _verdict(
            CONFLICT_DESTINATION_CONTENT_CHANGED, observed="b" * 64,
            occupant=None, reversal=None),
        CONFLICT_DESTINATION_MISSING_OR_MOVED: _verdict(
            CONFLICT_DESTINATION_MISSING_OR_MOVED, observed=None, occupant=None,
            reversal=None),
        CONFLICT_SOURCE_PATH_OCCUPIED: _verdict(
            CONFLICT_SOURCE_PATH_OCCUPIED, observed=FIXTURE_HASH,
            occupant="c" * 64, reversal=None),
        UNDO_REFUSED_UNAVAILABLE: _verdict(
            UNDO_REFUSED_UNAVAILABLE, observed=None, occupant=None,
            reversal=None),
    }


def hazard_root(root: Path) -> Mapping[str, Path]:
    """§8.3's hazards, as real objects, all beneath `root`.

    A protected container is CREATED but never opened by anything here: it is a
    directory with a `.app` name and one file inside, and P12's own refusal is
    reached from the name.
    """
    made: dict[str, Path] = {}

    ordinary = root / "ordinary.pdf"
    ordinary.write_bytes(b"an ordinary file")
    made["ordinary"] = ordinary

    link = root / "shortcut.pdf"
    link.symlink_to(ordinary)
    made["symlink"] = link

    bundle = root / "Numbers.app"
    (bundle / "Contents").mkdir(parents=True)
    (bundle / "Contents" / "sheet.numbers").write_bytes(b"never opened")
    made["package_bundle"] = bundle

    nfc = root / "Café.pdf"
    nfd = root / "Café.pdf"
    nfc.write_bytes(b"composed")
    made["unicode_nfc"] = nfc
    made["unicode_nfd"] = nfd

    case_upper = root / "Resume.pdf"
    case_upper.write_bytes(b"upper")
    made["case_upper"] = case_upper
    made["case_lower"] = root / "resume.pdf"

    long_directory = root / ("L" * 200)
    long_directory.mkdir()
    made["over_length_parent"] = long_directory
    made["over_length_name"] = long_directory / ("N" * 200 + ".pdf")

    made["reserved_name"] = root / "CON.txt"

    cloud = root / "CloudDrive"
    cloud.mkdir()
    (cloud / "notes.pdf").write_bytes(b"synced")
    (cloud / "notes (conflicted copy 2026-08-29).pdf").write_bytes(b"conflict")
    made["cloud_root"] = cloud
    made["cloud_conflict_copy"] = cloud / "notes (conflicted copy 2026-08-29).pdf"

    for name in ("VolumeA", "VolumeB"):
        volume = root / name
        volume.mkdir()
        made[name.lower()] = volume

    made["detached_volume"] = root / "DetachedVolume"
    return made


def _node(node_id: str, label: str, parent: str | None, *,
          node_type: str = "proposed", anchor: str = "root_documents",
          existing_path: str | None = None, ordinal: int = 0) -> Node:
    return Node(
        node_id=node_id, plan_version_id="plan-1", node_type=node_type,
        display_label=label, parent_node_id=parent, root_anchor=anchor,
        ordinal=ordinal, associated_group_ids=(),
        explanation="published P12 resolution fixture", node_role="ordinary",
        accepts_placement=True, handling_class="personal_non_sensitive",
        origin_node_id=node_id, existing_path=existing_path)


def resolution_fixtures() -> Mapping[str, tuple[Node, ...]]:
    """The three shapes the SPEC asks P12 to publish for P10 and P11."""
    return {
        "four_level_second_is_existing": (
            _node("n-career", "Career", None),
            _node("n-stripe", "Stripe", "n-career", node_type="existing",
                  existing_path="/fixture/Documents/Career/Stripe"),
            _node("n-2026", "2026 Job Search", "n-stripe"),
            _node("n-offers", "Offers", "n-2026"),
        ),
        "siblings_normalizing_to_one_name": (
            _node("n-career", "Career", None),
            _node("n-offers-upper", "Offers", "n-career", ordinal=0),
            _node("n-offers-lower", "offers", "n-career", ordinal=1),
        ),
        "source_and_destination_under_two_roots": (
            _node("n-downloads", "Downloads", None, anchor="root_downloads",
                  node_type="existing",
                  existing_path="/fixture/Downloads"),
            _node("n-projects", "Personal Projects", None,
                  anchor="root_desktop"),
        ),
    }
```

## What this plan does not build, and why

| Not built | Why |
|---|---|
| `src/mutation/stage_output.py` | **F3.** P2's `STAGE_IDS` has no mutation stage and adding one edits a P2 file (**A5**). |
| A `review_approvals` table | P13 owns the producer. P12 publishes the typed record and reads through an injected callable, so no source module impersonates P13. |
| A bundle-suffix table, a reserved-name table, a prohibited-character table, a suffix format, a root-anchor path map, a filing policy | **A7.** Every one is injected with no default, or imported from the part that already owns it (`scan_agent.exclusion` for protected containers). |
| A `SENSITIVE_CLASSES` set | **C5** is open. Publishing it would answer it in code. |
| Locked-file, open-file, alias and shortcut behaviour | **F5.** §8.3 demands a defined behaviour and supplies none. |
| An automatic rollback after a failed V3 or a failed batch | Undo is conditional and per-entry (§8.3); an all-or-nothing rollback contradicts it. |
| Any deletion of a user file | **§7.11**, and Task 14's guards assert it three ways. |
